```
=== README (first 120 lines) ===
# opencascade-binding-generator

Automatically generates Rust \`extern "C"\` FFI bindings for the [OpenCASCADE](https://dev.opencascade.org/) C++ CAD kernel.

Given a set of OCCT header files, the generator produces a complete Rust FFI layer: type declarations, method bindings, constructor wrappers, Handle smart pointer support, collection iterators, inheritance upcasts, and per-module re-exports with ergonomic short names.

## Quick Start

\`\`\`bash
# Regenerate all bindings (from repo root)
./scripts/regenerate-bindings.sh
\`\`\`

This parses OCCT headers configured in \`bindings.toml\` (expanding with automatic dependency resolution), and writes generated code to \`crates/opencascade-sys/generated/\`.

## CLI Usage

\`\`\`bash
# Standard regeneration (from repo root):
./scripts/regenerate-bindings.sh

# Manual invocation with TOML config:
cargo run -p opencascade-binding-generator -- \
    --config crates/opencascade-sys/bindings.toml \
    -I target/OCCT/include \
    -o crates/opencascade-sys/generated

# Or with explicit header arguments (legacy):
cargo run -p opencascade-binding-generator -- \
    -I target/OCCT/include \
    -o crates/opencascade-sys/generated \
    target/OCCT/include/gp_Pnt.hxx target/OCCT/include/TopoDS_Shape.hxx ...
\`\`\`

**Flags:**
- \`--config <file>\` — TOML configuration file specifying headers (recommended)
- \`--resolve-deps\` — Auto-include header dependencies (default: true)
- \`--dump-symbols\` — Dump symbol table for debugging
- \`--dry-run\` — Parse without generating
- \`-v, --verbose\` — Verbose output
- \`--module <name>\` — Filter to a specific module

## Generated Public API

Users interact with \`opencascade-sys\` through per-module re-exports. Each OCCT module (gp, TopoDS, BRepPrimAPI, etc.) becomes a Rust module with short type names and idiomatic method signatures.

### Geometry Primitives

Simple value types like points, vectors, and directions get constructors, accessors, and transformation methods. All methods go through extern "C" C++ wrappers. Methods that return class types by value return \`OwnedPtr<T>\`.

\`\`\`rust
use opencascade_sys::gp::{Pnt, Vec, Dir, Ax1, Ax2, Trsf};

// Constructors -- overloads distinguished by parameter type suffix
let origin = Pnt::new();                          // default constructor
let p = Pnt::new_real3(1.0, 2.0, 3.0);           // from coordinates
let p2 = Pnt::new_xyz(&xyz);                      // from XYZ

// Direct accessors
let x: f64 = p.x();
let y: f64 = p.y();
p.set_x(10.0);

// Transformations return OwnedPtr (by-value return needs C++ wrapper)
let mirrored: OwnedPtr<Pnt> = p.mirrored_pnt(&origin);
let rotated = p.rotated(&axis, std::f64::consts::FRAC_PI_2);
let transformed = p.transformed(&trsf);

// Clone into a new OwnedPtr
let p_copy = p.clone();

\`\`\`

### Topology Types and Inheritance

Topological shapes form an inheritance hierarchy: \`Shape\` is the base, with subtypes \`Vertex\`, \`Edge\`, \`Wire\`, \`Face\`, \`Shell\`, \`Solid\`, \`CompSolid\`, and \`Compound\`. Extern "C" FFI treats these as unrelated opaque types, so the generator produces explicit upcast methods (both const and mutable) and downcast free functions.

\`\`\`rust
use opencascade_sys::topo_ds::{Shape, Edge, Face, Solid, Wire};

// Upcasting -- every subtype can upcast to its ancestors
let edge = Edge::new();
let shape_ref: &Shape = edge.as_shape();                    // const upcast
let shape_mut: &mut Shape = edge.as_shape_mut();            // mutable upcast

// Downcasting -- free functions in the topo_ds module
// (wraps OCCT's TopoDS::Edge(), TopoDS::Face(), etc.)
use opencascade_sys::topo_ds;
let edge: OwnedPtr<Edge> = topo_ds::edge(&some_shape);       // const
let face: OwnedPtr<Face> = topo_ds::face_mut(&mut shape);    // mutable

// Copy semantics (TopoDS shapes are reference-counted internally)
let shape_copy = shape.clone();

\`\`\`

### Primitive Shape Construction (Builder Pattern)

OCCT builder classes follow a pattern: construct with parameters, then extract the result by upcasting to \`MakeShape\` and calling \`shape()\`.

\`\`\`rust
use opencascade_sys::gp::{Pnt, Ax2};
use opencascade_sys::b_rep_prim_api::{MakeBox, MakeSphere, MakeCylinder};

// Overloaded constructors use parameter type suffixes
let mut make_box = MakeBox::new_real3(10.0, 20.0, 30.0);    // dx, dy, dz
let mut make_box = MakeBox::new_pnt_real3(&p, 10.0, 20.0, 30.0); // corner + size
let mut make_box = MakeBox::new_pnt2(&p1, &p2);             // two corners
let mut make_box = MakeBox::new_ax2_real3(&axes, 10.0, 20.0, 30.0); // local coords

// Extract the result via mutable upcast to MakeShape
let make_shape = make_box.as_b_rep_builder_api_make_shape_mut();
let result: &TopoDS_Shape = make_shape.shape();
\`\`\`

### Boolean Operations

\`\`\`rust
use opencascade_sys::b_rep_algo_api::{Fuse, Cut, Common};
use opencascade_sys::message::ProgressRange;
```
</details>

```
807	        match self {
808	            Type::Void => "void".to_string(),

810	            Type::I32 => "int32_t".to_string(),
811	            Type::U32 => "uint32_t".to_string(),

825	            // when the inner type is itself a pointer (e.g., ConstRef(MutPtr(Class("X")))),
826	            // the const correctly qualifies the pointer level, not the pointee:

843	    /// represented in Rust FFI. Only catches template types with \`<>\`.
844	    /// Non-underscore class names (e.g., \`LDOMString\`) are NOT caught here —

846	    /// which checks against the symbol table.
847	    fn is_unresolved_template_type(&self) -> bool {

850	                // Template types with angle brackets are not representable

852	            }
853	            Type::ConstRef(inner) | Type::MutRef(inner) | Type::RValueRef(inner) | Type::ConstPtr(inner) | Type::MutPtr(inner) => {
```

> I see that the `element_type` is a raw C++ type string like "Standard_Boolean" for template containers. The goal is to replace deprecated symbols with their non-deprecated equivalents while keeping the same binding surface, and I'm starting to categorize these substitutions—beginning with `Standard_Integer` mapping to `int`.
> 
> For `Standard_Boolean`, it's also a pure alias that could map to `bool`, but there's a critical constraint: changing the element type string would alter the generated FFI symbol names and Rust type names (like `NCollection_HArray1_bool` instead of `NCollection_HArray1_Standard_Boolean`), which would break the public API. 396	    // Bridge the mangled canonical identifier to the C++ template spelling so the

```
        ));
        output.push_str(&format!(
            "extern \"C\" bool {handle_name}_is_null(const {handle_ctype}* self_) {{ return self_->IsNull(); }}\n\n"
        ));
    }

    output
```

```
/// Returns true if the element type is a C++ primitive that maps to a Rust native type.
pub fn is_primitive_element(cpp_type: &str) -> bool {
```

**After:**
```
/// Returns true if the element type is a C++ primitive that maps to a Rust native type.
pub fn is_primitive_element(cpp_type: &str) -> bool {

    matches!(
        cpp_type,
        "Standard_Real" | "double"
            | "Standard_Integer" | "int"
            | "Standard_Boolean" | "bool"
            | "Standard_ShortReal" | "float"
    )
}

/// Maps a deprecated OCCT primitive type alias to its canonical C++ spelling
/// (e.g. `Standard_Boolean` → `bool`), returning the input unchanged for anything
/// that is not a deprecated scalar alias.
///
/// This is applied only to the *C++ type spelling* emitted in wrapper bodies. The
/// FFI symbol names (`typedef_name`, `handle_name`, overload suffix tokens) and the
/// Rust element type are derived separately from the original spelling, so the
/// binding surface is unchanged — this only keeps the generated C++ free of
/// `-Wdeprecated-declarations` warnings. Every alias here is `Standard_DEPRECATED`
/// in `Standard_TypeDef.hxx` and is an exact `typedef` for the canonical type, so
/// substitution is ABI-identical.
pub fn canonical_cpp_primitive(cpp_type: &str) -> &str {
    match cpp_type {
        "Standard_Integer" => "int",
        "Standard_UInteger" => "unsigned int",
        "Standard_Real" => "double",
        "Standard_Boolean" => "bool",
        "Standard_ShortReal" => "float",
        "Standard_Character" => "char",
        "Standard_Byte" => "uint8_t",
        "Standard_Utf8Char" => "char",
        "Standard_Utf8UChar" => "unsigned char",
        "Standard_ExtCharacter" => "char16_t",
        "Standard_Utf16Char" => "char16_t",
        "Standard_Utf32Char" => "char32_t",
        "Standard_WideChar" => "wchar_t",
        "Standard_Size" => "size_t",
        other => other,
    }
}

/// Replace deprecated OCCT primitive aliases with their canonical C++ spellings
/// inside a full template spelling (e.g. `NCollection_HArray1<Standard_Boolean>` →
/// `NCollection_HArray1<bool>`). Matches only whole identifier tokens, so mangled
/// FFI names like `NCollection_HArray1_Standard_Boolean` (where the alias is joined
/// by `_`) are never touched — `\b` treats `_` as a word character.
pub fn canonicalize_cpp_spelling(spelling: &str) -> String {
    use std::sync::OnceLock;
    static RE: OnceLock<regex::Regex> = OnceLock::new();
    let re = RE.get_or_init(|| {
        regex::Regex::new(
            r"\b(Standard_Integer|Standard_UInteger|Standard_Real|Standard_Boolean|Standard_ShortReal|Standard_Character|Standard_Byte|Standard_Utf8Char|Standard_Utf8UChar|Standard_ExtCharacter|Standard_Utf16Char|Standard_Utf32Char|Standard_WideChar|Standard_Size)\b",
        )
        .unwrap()
    });
    re.replace_all(spelling, |caps: &regex::Captures| {
        canonical_cpp_primitive(&caps[1]).to_string()
    })
    .into_owned()
}
```

```
pub fn handle_type_name(cpp_name: &str) -> String {
    // Underscore-preserving handle names (e.g. Handle_Geom_Curve,
    // Handle_NCollection_HArray1_gp_Pnt) — this mirrors OCCT's own classic
    // `Handle_X` typedef spelling and keeps the underlying class name readable.
    format!("Handle_{}", cpp_name.replace("::", "_"))
}
```

**After:**
```
pub fn handle_type_name(cpp_name: &str) -> String {
    // Underscore-preserving handle names (e.g. Handle_Geom_Curve,
    // Handle_NCollection_HArray1_gp_Pnt) — this mirrors OCCT's own classic
    // `Handle_X` typedef spelling and keeps the underlying class name readable.
    format!("Handle_{}", cpp_name.replace("::", "_"))
}

/// The small, fixed set of OCCT classes whose classic `Handle_X` typedef is
/// `Standard_DEPRECATED`. These are the classes declared with
/// `DEFINE_STANDARD_HANDLE` (which emits a deprecated `Handle_X` typedef) rather
/// than the usual `DEFINE_STANDARD_RTTIEXT` (which emits no `Handle_X` at all, so
/// the generator's own typedef is the sole, non-deprecated definition).
///
/// As of OCCT 8.0 these are the only three such classes in the public headers
/// (`grep -rl DEFINE_STANDARD_HANDLE target/OCCT/include/*.hxx`, excluding the
/// macro definition in `Standard_Handle.hxx`). If a future OCCT bump adds more,
/// they will resume warning until added here; re-run that grep to refresh.
pub fn handle_typedef_is_deprecated(cpp_name: &str) -> bool {
    matches!(
        cpp_name,
        "Standard_Type" | "math_NotSquare" | "math_SingularMatrix"
    )
}

/// C++ type spelling for a handle to `cpp_name`, for use in generated wrapper
/// bodies. Normally this is the classic `Handle_X` typedef (readable, and defined
/// by the generator for every handle-able class). For the few classes whose
/// `Handle_X` typedef OCCT marks `Standard_DEPRECATED` (see
/// [`handle_typedef_is_deprecated`]), it returns the canonical
/// `opencascade::handle<X>` form instead — an ABI-identical spelling that avoids
/// `-Wdeprecated-declarations`. FFI *symbol* names keep using [`handle_type_name`].
pub fn handle_cpp_type(cpp_name: &str) -> String {
    if handle_typedef_is_deprecated(cpp_name) {
        format!("opencascade::handle<{}>", cpp_name)
    } else {
        handle_type_name(cpp_name)
    }
}
```
</details>

```
    // 9b. Handle downcast wrappers. As with upcasts, use canonical handle spellings
    // for the C++ types. The base side is the class being emitted (`ffi_cn`); the
    // derived side is `hdown.derived_class`.
    for hdown in &bindings.handle_downcasts {
        let derived_handle = type_mapping::handle_cpp_type(&hdown.derived_class);
        let base_handle = type_mapping::handle_cpp_type(ffi_cn);
        writeln!(
            output,
            "extern \"C\" OcctResult<{derived_handle}*> {fn_name}(const {base_handle}* self_) {{",
            fn_name = hdown.ffi_fn_name,
        )
        .unwrap();
        writeln!(output, "    try {{").unwrap();
        writeln!(
            output,
            "    opencascade::handle<{derived_class}> result = opencascade::handle<{derived_class}>::DownCast(*self_);",
            derived_class = hdown.derived_class
        )
        .unwrap();
        writeln!(output, "    if (result.IsNull()) {{ return {{nullptr, nullptr}}; }}").unwrap();
        writeln!(
            output,
            "    return {{new {derived_handle}(result), nullptr}};",
```

```markdown
name: deprecation-warnings-canonicalized
description: How cargo build's C++ -Wdeprecated-declarations warnings were cut from ~1082 to 30 without changing FFI surface
metadata:

---

`cargo build` emitted ~1082 C++ `-Wdeprecated-declarations` warnings from generated
wrappers. Fixed (2026-07-23) by canonicalizing deprecated OCCT type *spellings* in the
emitted C++ while keeping FFI *symbol names* unchanged → **1082 → 30**, zero surface
change (identical extern-C symbol set, zero generated `.rs` diff).

Mechanism:
- `codegen/collections.rs`: templates now emit `int` (not `Standard_Integer`) for
  index/length params. `canonical_cpp_primitive()` (bare type, e.g. `Standard_Boolean`→
  `bool`) + `canonicalize_cpp_spelling()` (regex whole-word, for `NCollection_HArray1<
  Standard_Boolean>`→`<bool>`) applied to element/value/cpp_spelling. Symbol names like
  `NCollection_HArray1_Standard_Boolean` are kept (the `_`-joined alias isn't a `\b` token).
- `type_mapping.rs`: `handle_cpp_type()` emits canonical `opencascade::handle<X>` in C++
  type positions for the classes whose `Handle_X` typedef OCCT marks deprecated, keeping
  `Handle_X` only as the FFI symbol prefix. Only **3** OCCT classes use the deprecating
  `DEFINE_STANDARD_HANDLE` macro (vs the usual `DEFINE_STANDARD_RTTIEXT`, which defines no
  `Handle_X`): `Standard_Type`, `math_NotSquare`, `math_SingularMatrix` — see
  `handle_typedef_is_deprecated()`. A redeclared typedef does NOT clear clang's deprecation,
  so the canonical spelling is the fix. Handle emission lives in TWO loops in `codegen/cpp.rs`
  (~line 767 and ~995) plus to_handle/get/upcast/downcast in `codegen/bindings.rs` — all patched.
- Preamble template-instantiation typedefs (`codegen/cpp.rs` `generate_preamble` + the
  in-file variant) also canonicalized.

**Irreducible remainder (30 warnings): `Standard_Mutex` (17) + `Transfer_TransferDeadLoop`
(13).** Both are fully-bound deprecated OCCT *classes* (auto-resolved deps, not in
bindings.toml), so there's no non-deprecated OCCT symbol to swap to: Standard_Mutex's
replacement is `std::mutex` (a non-OCCT stdlib type, different API); Transfer_TransferDeadLoop
is a dead exception with no replacement. Silencing them needs either exclusion (reduces
surface) or a scoped `#pragma`, not a symbol swap — left for the user to decide.

Related: [[collections-deprecated-aliases]], [[occt-8-binding-breakages-fixed]].
```

Done. Here's the summary.

## Result: 1079 → 30 deprecation warnings, zero binding-surface change

`cargo build`'s C++ `-Wdeprecated-declarations` warnings came entirely from generated wrappers spelling types with **deprecated OCCT aliases**. Where a non-deprecated, ABI-identical spelling exists, I swapped it in the emitted C++ while keeping every FFI **symbol name** unchanged.

| Deprecated symbol | Before | After | Fix |
|---|--:|--:|---|
| `Standard_Integer` | 1022 | **0** | collection templates emit `int` |
| `Standard_Boolean` | 15 | **0** | element/typedef spelling → `bool` |
| `Handle_Standard_Type` | 12 | **0** | emit `opencascade::handle<Standard_Type>` in C++ type positions |
| `Standard_Mutex` | 17 | 17 | *irreducible* |
| `Transfer_TransferDeadLoop` | 13 | 13 | *irreducible* |

### What changed (generator only)
- [collections.rs](crates/opencascade-binding-generator/src/codegen/collections.rs): `canonical_cpp_primitive()` + `canonicalize_cpp_spelling()` map deprecated primitive aliases to canonical C++ (`Standard_Boolean`→`bool`, etc.); array templates emit `int`.
- [type_mapping.rs](crates/opencascade-binding-generator/src/type_mapping.rs): `handle_cpp_type()` emits `opencascade::handle<X>` for the **only 3** OCCT classes whose `Handle_X` typedef is deprecated (`Standard_Type`, `math_NotSquare`, `math_SingularMatrix` — the ones using `DEFINE_STANDARD_HANDLE`). A redeclared typedef doesn't clear clang's deprecation, so the canonical spelling is the real fix.
- [cpp.rs](crates/opencascade-binding-generator/src/codegen/cpp.rs) + [bindings.rs](crates/opencascade-binding-generator/src/codegen/bindings.rs): applied at the handle-emission loops, preamble typedefs, and up/downcast/to_handle/get wrappers.

### Surface preservation — verified
- **Zero** generated `.rs` diff (the Rust FFI declarations user code links against are byte-identical).
- extern-C symbol set is **identical** (8511 symbols, `diff` empty). Only the C++ *implementation* spelling changed, e.g. `NCollection_HArray1_Standard_Boolean_ctor_int2(int …)` — same symbol, `int` instead of `Standard_Integer`.
- Full workspace `cargo build` clean; 22 generator tests + all workspace tests pass.

### The remaining 30 — genuinely can't be done under your constraint
`Standard_Mutex` and `Transfer_TransferDeadLoop` are fully-bound deprecated OCCT **classes** (pulled in as auto-resolved deps). There's no non-deprecated *OCCT* symbol to swap to: Standard_Mutex's replacement is `std::mutex` (a stdlib type with a different API, not an OCCT class), and Transfer_TransferDeadLoop is a dead exception with no replacement. So they can't be silenced by "changing to a non-deprecated symbol" — only by **excluding** them (reduces surface) or wrapping their wrappers in a scoped `#pragma clang diagnostic ignored`  (suppression, not a swap). I left both alone since each crosses a line you drew.

Want me to pursue either option for those last 30 (e.g. a scoped pragma, which keeps the surface intact), or leave them as-is? Note: I haven't committed anything.

## 🤖 Assistant
<!-- msg_011CdZUnZVJwmXQSQDsb5eWj -->

No — and the evidence is unusually clear-cut on both halves of your question.

## Nothing requires them

Neither type appears in **any** other public OCCT header — not as a parameter, return type, base class, or member. There isn't even an `#include` of `Standard_Mutex.hxx` or `Transfer_TransferDeadLoop.hxx` anywhere in the installed headers. They're isolated leaves, so no other binding can reach them and excluding them can't cut off access to anything else.

## OCCT itself doesn't use them

- **`Transfer_TransferDeadLoop`**: **zero** references in the entire OCCT source tree outside its own `.hxx`/`.cxx` and a `FILES.cmake` listing. It is never thrown, exactly as its deprecation message says. A client cannot catch what is never raised. Bound surface: 3 wrappers (ctor, destructor, `exception_type`).
- **`Standard_Mutex`**: only 3 files mention it — its own `.hxx`, `.cxx`, and `FILES.cmake`. OCCT has fully migrated off it internally. Bound surface: 5 wrappers (ctor/lock/try_lock/unlock/destructor) plus `Sentry`.

## Would a client want them directly?

For `Standard_Mutex`, no — and this is the case where "no non-deprecated alternative" is misleading. There's no OCCT *capability* gated behind it; it's just a mutex. The replacement is `std::mutex`, and a Rust client should reach for `std::sync::Mutex`, which is strictly safer than driving a C++ mutex across FFI: the guard is tied to the data, and there's no manual `lock`/`unlock` pair that can be skipped on an early return or unwind. Exposing raw lock/unlock over FFI is arguably a footgun rather than a feature.

For `Transfer_TransferDeadLoop`, also no — a dead exception type.

## A relevant precedent in your own generator

The parser **already skips deprecated methods and constructors** ([parser.rs:705](crates/opencascade-binding-generator/src/parser.rs:705), [:863](crates/opencascade-binding-generator/src/parser.rs:863), [:906](crates/opencascade-binding-generator/src/parser.rs:906)) via `Availability::Deprecated`. So "don't bind deprecated things" is established policy — it just isn't applied at *class* granularity. Both of these are class-level `Standard_DEPRECATED`, which clang reports the same way.

Blast radius is tiny: only 5 headers in all of OCCT have a class-level `Standard_DEPRECATED`, and of those you bind exactly these 2. `BRepMesh_FastDiscret` and `Message_ProgressSentry` aren't bound at all, and the deprecated `TCollection` class isn't used either (the 282 `TCollection_*` symbols are `TCollection_AsciiString`/`ExtendedString` — different classes, and they produce no warnings).

## Recommendation

Given your goal is a *more complete* FFI layer, I'd go with the **scoped `#pragma clang diagnostic push/ignored("-Wdeprecated-declarations")/pop`** around those two wrapper blocks: it reaches 0 warnings while keeping every symbol, and it's future-proof for OCCT's ongoing deprecation campaign — when they deprecate something genuinely useful, you'll want the binding to survive without noise.

Extending the existing deprecation skip to classes is the tidier alternative and matches your current policy, but it does trade away 8 wrappers. Since those 8 are provably dead, that trade is defensible — it just isn't the "keep the surface" answer you set out to get.

Want me to implement either one? Both are small generator changes.

## 🤖🔧 Assistant
<details>
<summary>Write tmp/commit-msg.txt</summary>

```
Eliminate -Wdeprecated-declarations warnings in generated C++ (1079 -> 0)

`cargo build` emitted 1079 -Wdeprecated-declarations warnings from the generated
C++ wrappers. Two independent causes, fixed separately.

1. Deprecated type *spellings* (1079 -> 30), no FFI surface change

The wrappers spelled types using OCCT scalar aliases (Standard_Integer,
Standard_Boolean) and the classic Handle_X typedefs, all of which OCCT 8.0 marks
`Standard_DEPRECATED`. Emit the canonical spelling in wrapper bodies instead:

  - `int` / `bool` for the scalar aliases. collections.rs gains
    `canonical_cpp_primitive` (bare type) and `canonicalize_cpp_spelling`
    (whole-word regex, for template spellings like
    `NCollection_HArray1<Standard_Boolean>` -> `<bool>`).
  - `opencascade::handle<X>` for the deprecated `Handle_X` typedefs, via
    `type_mapping::handle_cpp_type`. Only three OCCT classes declare their handle
    with the deprecating `DEFINE_STANDARD_HANDLE` macro (Standard_Type,
    math_NotSquare, math_SingularMatrix); everything else uses
    DEFINE_STANDARD_RTTIEXT, which defines no Handle_X at all, so the generator's
    own typedef is the sole, non-deprecated definition. Redeclaring the typedef
    does not clear clang's deprecation attribute, so the canonical spelling is
    the only fix. Handle wrappers are emitted from two loops in codegen/cpp.rs
    plus to_handle/get/upcast/downcast in codegen/bindings.rs; all are covered.

extern-C symbol names are derived separately (handle_type_name, typedef_name,
overload suffix tokens) and keep the original spelling, so this part is a pure
spelling change: the exported symbol set is identical and the generated .rs files
have a zero diff. Every substitution is an exact typedef, hence ABI-identical.

2. Deprecated *classes* (30 -> 0), small provably-dead surface removal

The remaining 30 warnings came from Standard_Mutex and Transfer_TransferDeadLoop,
which are deprecated OCCT classes rather than aliases — there is no
non-deprecated symbol to swap to. The parser already skips deprecated functions,
constructors and methods via `Availability::Deprecated`; apply the same rule at
class granularity in `parse_class`. Returning early also drops nested classes
(Standard_Mutex::Sentry), which are deprecated along with their parent.

This drops exactly 23 extern-C symbols (12 Standard_Mutex incl. Sentry, 11
Transfer_TransferDeadLoop) and adds none. The removed surface is dead:

  - Neither type is referenced by any other installed OCCT header — not as a
    parameter, return type, base class or member — and no header #includes
    either one. They are isolated leaves, so nothing else can need them.
  - Transfer_TransferDeadLoop has zero references anywhere in the OCCT source
    tree outside its own files; it is never thrown, matching its deprecation
    note. It cannot be caught, so binding it buys nothing.
  - Standard_Mutex is mentioned only by its own .hxx/.cxx; OCCT no longer uses it
    internally. Its documented replacement is std::mutex, and Rust clients have
    std::sync::Mutex, which is safer than driving a C++ mutex over FFI.

The parent classes stay bound, so exception coverage is unaffected:
Standard_Failure, Transfer_TransferFailure (the exception actually thrown),
Interface_InterfaceError and Standard_ErrorHandler all remain. No dangling
references to the removed types remain in the generated output.

Only 5 installed headers carry a class-level Standard_DEPRECATED; of those just
these 2 were bound (BRepMesh_FastDiscret and Message_ProgressSentry are not
bound, and the deprecated TCollection class is unused), so the new rule has no
other effect today.

Verification: `cargo build` reports 0 deprecation warnings (the 3 remaining
-Wdelete-incomplete warnings are pre-existing and unrelated); 22 generator tests
and the full workspace test suite pass.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 <noreply@anthropic.com>

```
</details>

```
16	- \`codegen/collections.rs\`: templates now emit \`int\` (not \`Standard_Integer\`) for
17	  index/length params. \`canonical_cpp_primitive()\` (bare type, e.g. \`Standard_Boolean\`→
18	  \`bool\`) + \`canonicalize_cpp_spelling()\` (regex whole-word, for \`NCollection_HArray1<
19	  Standard_Boolean>\`→\`<bool>\`) applied to element/value/cpp_spelling. Symbol names like
20	  \`NCollection_HArray1_Standard_Boolean\` are kept (the \`_\`-joined alias isn't a \`\b\` token).
21	- \`type_mapping.rs\`: \`handle_cpp_type()\` emits canonical \`opencascade::handle<X>\` in C++
22	  type positions for the classes whose \`Handle_X\` typedef OCCT marks deprecated, keeping
23	  \`Handle_X\` only as the FFI symbol prefix. Only **3** OCCT classes use the deprecating
24	  \`DEFINE_STANDARD_HANDLE\` macro (vs the usual \`DEFINE_STANDARD_RTTIEXT\`, which defines no
25	  \`Handle_X\`): \`Standard_Type\`, \`math_NotSquare\`, \`math_SingularMatrix\` — see
26	  \`handle_typedef_is_deprecated()\`. A redeclared typedef does NOT clear clang's deprecation,
27	  so the canonical spelling is the fix. Handle emission lives in TWO loops in \`codegen/cpp.rs\`
28	  (~line 767 and ~995) plus to_handle/get/upcast/downcast in \`codegen/bindings.rs\` — all patched.

30	  in-file variant) also canonicalized.
31

35	replacement is \`std::mutex\` (a non-OCCT stdlib type, different API); Transfer_TransferDeadLoop
```

## 🤖 Assistant
<!-- msg_011CdZVXDM3y45nLQeEh2jZj -->

Done. **Deprecation warnings are now at zero**, and the commit is amended.

## The change

Extended the parser's existing deprecation skip to class granularity in [parser.rs](crates/opencascade-binding-generator/src/parser.rs) — `parse_class` now returns early on `Availability::Deprecated`, exactly as it already did for functions, constructors, and methods. The early return also drops nested classes, which handles `Standard_Mutex::Sentry` for free.

## Impact — measured, not assumed

- **23 extern-C symbols removed, 0 added** — 12 from `Standard_Mutex` (incl. `Sentry`), 11 from `Transfer_TransferDeadLoop`. No collateral loss anywhere else.
- **Parent classes intact**: `Standard_Failure`, `Transfer_TransferFailure` (the exception that *is* actually thrown), `Interface_InterfaceError`, `Standard_ErrorHandler` all still bound. Zero dangling references to the removed types.
- Only 5 installed headers carry a class-level `Standard_DEPRECATED`; just these 2 were bound, so the new rule has no other effect today.

## Warnings, precisely

The original 1082 figure in the old commit message was slightly off — it was clang's total, comprising **1079 `-Wdeprecated-declarations` + 3 `-Wdelete-incomplete`**. So the accurate result is **1079 → 0** deprecation warnings. The 3 `-Wdelete-incomplete` remain; they're pre-existing and unrelated, and I said so in the commit message rather than claiming a clean zero.

## Verification

`scripts/regenerate-bindings.sh` → `cargo build` (0 deprecation warnings) → 22 generator tests pass → full workspace suite passes, 0 failures.

## Commit

Amended to `d8c58a2`, working tree clean. The message now covers both halves as one coherent story: part 1 (spelling canonicalization, zero surface change) and part 2 (deprecated-class skip), including the evidence that the removed surface is dead — no other header references or includes either type, `Transfer_TransferDeadLoop` has zero references in all of OCCT's source, and `Standard_Mutex` is mentioned only by its own files.

One forward-looking note I put in the commit and memory: this rule is right *because* these two were dead. If OCCT later deprecates a class that's genuinely useful, the better move is a scoped `#pragma clang diagnostic ignored` around its wrappers rather than silently losing the binding — otherwise the skip will quietly erode surface as OCCT's deprecation campaign continues.

```
10680	> Looking at the NURBS corpus data, a significant portion of each model's surface is analytic geometry where normal measurements can't be computed — ranging from 32% to 66% depending on the model, with \`elliptical_cylinder\` being particularly problematic at only 32.5% measurable. The freeform regions are where the most challenging problems cluster, like the blister conditioning issues and the degenerate geometry in \`hill_with_hole\`.
10681	> 
10682	> The real blockers turned out to be missing bindings in the OCCT wrapper — \`Geom_SurfaceResD1\`/\`Curve_ResD1\` are inaccessible, \`GeomLProp_SLProps\` is just a type alias with no implementation, and \`TopTools_IndexedMapOfShape\` doesn't exist. But none of these actually prevented the work; the \`d1\` accessor exists and works around the first issue. The actual limitation — excluding freeform reference faces — was my implementation choice, and it's now removable using \`par_on_face_s2\` plus the \`d1\` accessor, though it would cost 34–68% of the NURBS corpus surface area. The \`analytic_area_fraction\` metric is doing its job by flagging this gap on every run, and it's worth offering to fix since it's clearly valuable and relatively small in scope.

10693	| \`Geom_Surface_ResD1\` / \`Curve_ResD1\` | type bound, **zero accessors** (only \`new()\`) | makes \`eval_d1\`/\`eval_d2\` unusable, but redundant given \`d1\` |
10694	| \`GeomLProp_SLProps\` | **bare type alias, no impl** ([geom_l_prop.rs:230](../opencascade-rs/crates/opencascade-sys/generated/geom_l_prop.rs:230)) — no ctor, no \`Normal()\` | none — also redundant given \`d1\` |
10695	| \`TopTools_IndexedMapOfShape\` | **absent** (\`top_tools.rs\` binds only \`LocationSet\`/\`ShapeSet\`) | I used a linear \`is_same\` scan for shape→index; 26–71 pointer comparisons per query, negligible beside the distance call it follows |
10696	
10697	The closed-form analytic normals stay the right choice regardless — for the five analytic types the normal at a sample point equals the normal at its footpoint, so no \`(u,v)\` and no surface evaluation is needed at all, which is what the module docs record.
10698	
10699	## But the correction exposes a real limitation I introduced
10700	
10701	I excluded \`RefSurface::Other\` — B-spline, Bezier, revolution, extrusion, offset — from the normal report because there's no closed form. I'd assumed that was forced. It isn't: \`par_on_face_s2\` gives the footpoint's \`(u,v)\` on the winning reference face, and \`d1\` gives the normal there as \`D1U × D1V\`. So freeform reference faces are scoreable, and I chose not to.
10702	
10703	That costs more than I'd have guessed. On the NURBS corpus:
10704	
10705	| model | analytic area |
10706	|---|---|
10707	| \`elliptical_cylinder\` | 32.5% |
10708	| \`elliptical_arch\` | 56.9% |
10709	| \`bspline_hill\` | 58.9% |
10710	| \`hill_with_hole\` | 62.1% |
10711	| \`bspline_saddle\` | 65.5% |
10712	
10713	So on those models **34–68% of the surface gets no normal measurement** — and the freeform half is exactly where the open problems are (Phase 5a's blister/saddle conditioning, hill_with_hole's junk torus). The \`analytic area\` line I print is doing its job of announcing this on every run, but it's announcing a gap that doesn't need to exist.
10714	
10715	One thing that did carry through to the freeform corpus: **the seam assumption reads 0.0000% on all 8 NURBS models**, so B-spline face boundaries are respected as cleanly as analytic ones.
10716	
10717	Want me to extend the normal report to freeform reference faces via \`par_on_face_s2\` + \`d1\`? It's contained — read back the \`(u,v)\` I currently discard, evaluate, and drop the \`Other\` early-return — and it would make the instrument cover whole models rather than their analytic fraction. Separately, \`GeomLProp_SLProps\` and the \`ResD1\` accessors are worth filing against \`../opencascade-rs\` as binding bugs even though nothing here needs them.
```

<!-- msg_011CdZWeFfmMkTAMXt413Q4E -->

> Good — I've confirmed the orphan count is exactly 9, matching the four `find_index` calls and five `contains` calls I found. Now I'm ready to compile the final verdict on what the log actually claims versus what I verified.
> 
> The key insight is that `ResD1` has no accessors because it's a new OCCT 8.0 struct with class-type fields (gp_Pnt and gp_Vec), which fails the POD-struct check that gates the field-accessor generation path. The generator only maps scalar primitives, so even if the struct passed the POD check, those vector types wouldn't have Rust bindings. And `TopTools_IndexedMapOfShape` does exist in the bindings, just under a different mangled name. The `find_index` workaround was necessary, but not for the reason they initially thought — it's a real limitation of the binding generation, not a missing type.
> 
> For `SLProps`, the issue is that OCCT 8.0 converted these from plain classes to template aliases, which the parser skips entirely. Even though the type shows up as an opaque struct with a destructor (because other signatures reference it), no methods are ever parsed or generated. The `template_instantiations` config doesn't help either — it registers the type and emits a typedef but produces zero method bindings.
> 
> For `IndexedMapOfShape`, it's not actually missing. The old typedef was deliberately dropped when OCCT 8.0 pivoted to using the canonical `NCollection_IndexedMap<TopoDS_Shape, TopTools_ShapeMapHasher>` type, which is already bound with all the expected methods like `new`, `size`, `clear`, `add`, `find_key`, and iteration support.
> 
> The real issue is that `find_index` — the operation to look up a shape's index — gets generated in C++ but has no corresponding Rust extern declaration. The C++ emitter creates these wrappers for `IndexedMap`, but the Rust extern emitter only declares `add` and `find_key`, leaving about 9 orphaned C++ wrappers (4 `find_index` calls and 5 `contains` calls) without Rust bindings.
> 
> For brepper's immediate needs: the linear scan workaround stays necessary for now, but it's just a 3-line generator fix away — adding the missing `find_index` and `contains` declarations to the Rust side. They should specifically use the hasher variant of `IndexedMap` (`TopTools_IndexedMapOfShape` with `TopTools_ShapeMapHasher`) to ensure correct shape hashing in OCCT 8.0.
> 
> The user asked me to analyze whether the missing bindings claim is accurate and explain why they're missing, so I'll focus on that verdict and root causes rather than implementing the fix myself, though I can offer it as a next step. ShapeSet" is correct, but I was wrong to call it absent—it's actually there. I'll write the response now without an artifact since this is a straightforward technical clarification that works better in plain markdown.


## 🤖 Assistant
<!-- msg_011CdZWeFfmMkTAMXt413Q4E -->

Read it. Three of the four claims hold; one is wrong, and the workaround it justified was still necessary — for a different reason than the log gives.

## Verdict

| Log's claim | Verdict |
|---|---|
| `BRepAdaptor_Surface::D1` exists (its own self-correction) | ✅ Correct — `b_rep_adaptor.rs` has the 5-arg out-param form |
| `Geom_Surface_ResD1` / `Curve_ResD1`: type bound, zero accessors | ✅ Correct — only `new()` |
| `GeomLProp_SLProps`: bare type alias, no impl | ✅ Correct — one `destructor` extern, nothing else. Same for `BRepLProp_SLProps` |
| `TopTools_IndexedMapOfShape`: **absent** | ❌ **Wrong** — it's bound, under a different name |
| *(missed)* `find_index` unreachable from Rust | The linear scan really was needed — but because of a codegen bug, not a missing type |

## Why each one is missing

**1. `ResD1` — the generator can't express structs of non-scalar fields.**

It's a new OCCT 8.0 aggregate with public data members and no methods:
```cpp
struct ResD1 { gp_Pnt Point; gp_Vec D1U; gp_Vec D1V; };
```
The generator *does* have a field-accessor path, but it's gated on `is_pod_struct`, which requires `fields.iter().all(|f| f.ty.is_pod_field_type())` — all fields must be POD **primitives** ([parser.rs:1030](crates/opencascade-binding-generator/src/parser.rs:1030)). `gp_Pnt`/`gp_Vec` are class types, so the gate fails. Even past it, `pod_field_rust_type` maps only scalars and returns `None` for class types ([bindings.rs:2206](crates/opencascade-binding-generator/src/codegen/bindings.rs:2206)). So a struct of scalars gets accessors; a struct of OCCT value types becomes opaque. That's the whole `EvalD1`/`EvalD2`/`EvalD3` family.

**2. `SLProps` — it became a template in OCCT 8.0.**

These are no longer classes:
```cpp
using GeomLProp_SLProps = GeomLProp_SLPropsBase<occ::handle<Geom_Surface>>;
using BRepLProp_SLProps = GeomLProp_SLPropsBase<BRepAdaptor_Surface>;
```
The parser skips template classes outright ([parser.rs:743](crates/opencascade-binding-generator/src/parser.rs:743)), so `Normal()` is never parsed. The type survives only as an opaque struct because other signatures mention it.

Worth knowing before anyone files this as a quick fix: the `template_instantiations` config **won't** solve it. I checked against the existing `BVH_Builder<double, 3>` entry — it registers the type and emits a typedef/handle but produces **zero** method externs. Real work is needed (parse the template body with type-argument substitution), so this is the expensive one of the three.

**3. `TopTools_IndexedMapOfShape` — not missing, renamed.**

In OCCT 8.0 it's a deprecated typedef:
```cpp
typedef NCollection_IndexedMap<TopoDS_Shape, TopTools_ShapeMapHasher> TopTools_IndexedMapOfShape;
```
and the canonical type **is** bound, as `n_collection::IndexedMap_TopoDS_Shape_TopTools_ShapeMapHasher`, with `new/size/clear/add/find_key/iter`. The log grepped for the old alias spelling, which was deliberately dropped in the 8.0 alias→canonical pivot. `top_tools.rs` binding only `LocationSet`/`ShapeSet` is true but is the wrong place to look. Use the hasher variant, not the plain `IndexedMap_TopoDS_Shape` — `TopTools_ShapeMapHasher` is what gives OCCT's shape-identity semantics.

## The thing the log missed

The linear scan was still unavoidable — because `find_index`, the exact shape→index operation, is **generated in C++ but has no Rust declaration**. In [collections.rs](crates/opencascade-binding-generator/src/codegen/collections.rs), the C++ emitter's `IndexedMap` arm emits `add`/`find_key`/`find_index` (~line 765) and the `Map` arm emits `contains` (~line 629), but the Rust extern emitter's `IndexedMap | Map` arm (~line 1267) emits only `add` and `find_key`.

I diffed every C++ extern symbol against every Rust `pub fn` across the generated tree: **exactly 9 orphaned wrappers**, and they are precisely these — 4 `find_index` and 5 `contains`, including `NCollection_IndexedMap_TopoDS_Shape_TopTools_ShapeMapHasher_find_index`. Nothing else in the C++ layer is unreachable. So this is a contained, real bug, and it's the one that actually cost brepper something.

## Ranking, if you want fixes

1. **`find_index`/`contains` externs** — a few lines in that Rust arm; unblocks O(1) shape lookup and clears all 9 orphans.
2. **`ResD1` accessors** — needs the field path to handle class-typed fields (return `&gp_Pnt` etc. rather than by-value scalars). Moderate, and `d1()` already covers the use case.
3. **`SLProps`** — needs template-instantiation method generation. Largest, and redundant with `d1()`.

I'd also add the `TopTools_*` → `n_collection::*` mapping to [PORTING.md](crates/opencascade-sys/PORTING.md); this is the second time that alias pivot has read as "binding missing," which is exactly what that file is for.

Want me to do #1? It's small and I can verify it end-to-end with a regenerate-and-build.

```
//! Tests for POD (Plain Old Data) struct bindings.
//!
//! These tests verify that transparent \`#[repr(C)]\` Rust structs match
//! the C++ layout and can be used to read/write fields directly.

use opencascade_sys::bop_algo::MakePeriodic_PeriodicityParams;

/// Verify that the Rust struct size matches the C++ sizeof for each POD type.
/// This catches layout mismatches from field ordering, alignment, or padding
/// differences between the Rust and C++ definitions.
///
/// PeriodicityParams has: bool[3] + f64[3] + bool[3] + f64[3]
/// = 3*1 + 5(pad) + 3*8 + 3*1 + 5(pad) + 3*8 = 64 bytes
/// (with typical alignment: bools pack to 3 bytes, then 5 padding to align f64)
#[test]
fn periodicity_params_sizeof() {
    let rust_size = std::mem::size_of::<MakePeriodic_PeriodicityParams>();
    // The struct has 4 array fields: bool[3], f64[3], bool[3], f64[3]
    // Minimum size is 3 + 24 + 3 + 24 = 54 bytes, but alignment may add padding
    assert!(
        rust_size >= 54,
        "Rust sizeof ({}) is suspiciously small for PeriodicityParams (expected >= 54)",
        rust_size
    );
}
```

```
//! Tests for the NCollection map lookup methods (`find_index` / `contains`).
//!
//! The C++ wrappers for these have always been generated, but the Rust side
//! omitted the declarations, so they were unreachable. These tests exercise the
//! real OCCT containers to confirm the bindings link and return OCCT's semantics
//! (1-based indices, 0 for "not found").

use opencascade_sys::b_rep_prim_api::MakeBox;
use opencascade_sys::n_collection::{
    IndexedMap_TopoDS_Shape_TopTools_ShapeMapHasher, Map_TopoDS_Shape_TopTools_ShapeMapHasher,
};

/// Build two distinct solids to use as map keys.
fn two_shapes() -> (
    opencascade_sys::OwnedPtr<opencascade_sys::topo_ds::Shape>,
    opencascade_sys::OwnedPtr<opencascade_sys::topo_ds::Shape>,
) {
    let mut a = MakeBox::new_real3(10.0, 10.0, 10.0);
    let shape_a = a.as_b_rep_builder_api_make_shape_mut().shape().clone();
    let mut b = MakeBox::new_real3(20.0, 5.0, 5.0);
    let shape_b = b.as_b_rep_builder_api_make_shape_mut().shape().clone();
    (shape_a, shape_b)
}

/// `find_index` is the inverse of `find_key`: it maps a shape back to its
/// 1-based position, which is the whole reason to prefer an IndexedMap over a
/// linear scan.
#[test]
fn indexed_map_find_index_round_trips() {
    let (shape_a, shape_b) = two_shapes();
    let mut map = IndexedMap_TopoDS_Shape_TopTools_ShapeMapHasher::new();

    let idx_a = map.add(&shape_a);
    let idx_b = map.add(&shape_b);
    assert_eq!(map.size(), 2, "both shapes should be stored");
    assert_eq!(idx_a, 1, "OCCT indices are 1-based");
    assert_eq!(idx_b, 2);

    // find_index recovers the index that add() returned.
    assert_eq!(map.find_index(&shape_a), idx_a);
    assert_eq!(map.find_index(&shape_b), idx_b);

    // ...and round-trips through find_key back to the same shape.
    let recovered = map.find_key(map.find_index(&shape_b));
    assert_eq!(
        map.find_index(recovered),
        idx_b,
        "find_key(find_index(s)) should identify the same entry"
    );
}

/// OCCT returns 0 (not an error) for a key that is absent.
#[test]
fn indexed_map_find_index_returns_zero_when_absent() {
    let (shape_a, shape_b) = two_shapes();
    let mut map = IndexedMap_TopoDS_Shape_TopTools_ShapeMapHasher::new();
    map.add(&shape_a);

    assert_eq!(
        map.find_index(&shape_b),
        0,
        "absent key must yield 0, not a panic or a stale index"
    );
}

/// A plain Map has no index, so membership is the only lookup it offers.
#[test]
fn map_contains_reports_membership() {
    let (shape_a, shape_b) = two_shapes();
    let mut map = Map_TopoDS_Shape_TopTools_ShapeMapHasher::new();
    map.add(&shape_a);

    assert!(map.contains(&shape_a), "added shape should be present");
    assert!(!map.contains(&shape_b), "unadded shape should be absent");

    map.add(&shape_b);
    assert!(map.contains(&shape_b));
    assert_eq!(map.size(), 2);
}

```
</details>

> I could use `shape.oriented(shape.orientation())` to create an owned copy with the same orientation, since `oriented()` returns an `OwnedPtr<Shape>` and `TopoDS_Shape` is just a lightweight handle. But actually, there's a simpler approach — I can keep the `MakeBox` builders in scope and work directly with `&Shape` references, since the map methods accept references anyway.
> 
> The key insight is that the builders need to outlive the shape references. Instead of returning shapes from a helper function (which would let the builders go out of scope), I'll build everything inside each test, keeping the builders as local variables. When I call `as_b_rep_builder_api_make_shape_mut()`, it returns a mutable reference to the builder, and then `.shape()` gives me a `&Shape` tied to that borrow — so as long as I don't touch the builder again while holding the reference, I can use multiple shape references from different builders without issue.

```
    let mut box_b = MakeBox::new_real3(20.0, 5.0, 5.0);
```

<details>
<summary>Tool Result</summary>

```
### 1. Collections moved to canonical \`n_collection::\` names
OCCT 8.0 deprecated the \`TColgp_*\` / \`TColStd_*\` / \`TopTools_*\` collection aliases and
uses the canonical \`NCollection_*<T>\` templates directly. The bindings follow suit — every
collection now lives in the \`n_collection\` module named after the template + element:

| Old | New |
|-----|-----|
| \`t_colgp::Array1OfPnt\` | \`n_collection::Array1_gp_Pnt\` |
| \`t_colgp::Array2OfPnt\` | \`n_collection::Array2_gp_Pnt\` |
| \`t_colgp::Array1OfPnt2d\` | \`n_collection::Array1_gp_Pnt2d\` |
| \`t_col_std::Array1OfReal\` | \`n_collection::Array1_double\` |
| \`top_tools::ListOfShape\` | \`n_collection::List_TopoDS_Shape\` |
| \`top_tools::SequenceOfShape\` | \`n_collection::Sequence_TopoDS_Shape\` |
| \`top_tools::IndexedMapOfShape\` | \`n_collection::IndexedMap_TopoDS_Shape\` |

Shape-keyed maps carry the hasher in their name, e.g. a \`TopExp::MapShapes\` out-parameter is
\`n_collection::IndexedMap_TopoDS_Shape_TopTools_ShapeMapHasher\` (not the bare \`IndexedMap_TopoDS_Shape\`).
Collections of scalars are supported too (\`List_int\`, \`Map_int\`, \`Sequence_double\`,
\`DataMap_TopoDS_Shape_int\`, …); scalar map lookups return \`Option<T>\` by value.
```
</details>

## 🤖 Assistant
<!-- msg_011CdZXpHur4AsFDbvWRL7T8 -->

All green, including the new 3-test file. Writing the commit message:


## 🤖🔧 Assistant
<details>
<summary>Write tmp/commit-msg2.txt</summary>

```
Expose IndexedMap::find_index and Map::contains in the Rust bindings

The C++ collection emitter has always generated `find_index` for
`NCollection_IndexedMap` and `contains` for `NCollection_Map`, but the Rust
extern emitter's `IndexedMap | Map` arm declared only `add` and `find_key`. The
wrappers therefore existed in the compiled C++ with nothing on the Rust side able
to call them.

Diffing every extern-C symbol in generated/*.cpp against every `pub fn` in
generated/*.rs shows this was the *only* place the two sides disagreed: 9
orphaned wrappers, 4 `_find_index` (IndexedMap) and 5 `_contains` (Map). After
this change the two sides match exactly.

The gap had a real cost. `find_index` is the inverse of `find_key` and the whole
reason to reach for an IndexedMap: without it, mapping a shape back to its index
requires a linear `is_same` scan over the map, which is what a downstream client
ended up writing after concluding the type was unbound. The type was bound; only
this lookup was unreachable.

Add the missing declarations to `generate_rust_ffi_collections` (extern block)
and the matching safe wrappers to the `IndexedMap | Map` arm in codegen/rust.rs,
mirroring what the C++ side emits per kind. IndexedMap gains `find_index`, Map
gains `contains`; both take `&T` and follow OCCT semantics (1-based indices, 0
for "not found"). This adds 52 methods across the generated collections and
removes nothing.

Also add tests/collection_lookup.rs, which exercises the new methods against real
OCCT containers rather than just checking that they compile: index round-trip
through find_key/find_index, 0 for an absent key, and membership on a plain Map.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 <noreply@anthropic.com>

```
</details>

<summary>Tool Result</summary>

## 🤖 Assistant
<!-- msg_011CdaBobhpQRx62mj3KEyVY -->

Committed. Purely additive: 5 files, 238 insertions, 0 deletions.

## What was wrong

The C++ emitter and the Rust emitter disagreed about what an `IndexedMap`/`Map` exposes. C++ emitted `add`/`find_key`/`find_index` for IndexedMap and `add`/`contains` for Map; the Rust arm declared only `add` and `find_key`. So the wrappers were compiled into the library with nothing on the Rust side able to reach them.

## The fix

- [collections.rs](crates/opencascade-binding-generator/src/codegen/collections.rs) — added the missing extern declarations, with a comment noting the two arms must mirror each other since a mismatch silently orphans a wrapper.
- [rust.rs](crates/opencascade-binding-generator/src/codegen/rust.rs) — added the matching safe wrappers: `find_index` for IndexedMap, `contains` for Map.

Adds 52 methods across the generated collections. The orphan diff (every extern-C symbol vs. every Rust `pub fn`) now comes back empty — the 9 orphans are gone and nothing else in the C++ layer is unreachable.

## Verification

I didn't stop at "it compiles." [tests/collection_lookup.rs](crates/opencascade-sys/tests/collection_lookup.rs) drives the new methods against real OCCT containers — two boxes into a shape map, checking the index round-trip through `find_key`/`find_index`, that an absent key yields `0` rather than panicking or returning a stale index, and membership on a plain `Map`. All 3 pass, which also proves the externs link.

Full protocol green: regenerate → build (0 deprecation warnings, so the earlier work still holds) → 22 generator tests → full workspace suite, 0 failures.

## For brepper

The linear `is_same` scan can now go:

```bash
rg -n "is_same" ../brepper/src/stage3.rs
```

The replacement is `n_collection::IndexedMap_TopoDS_Shape_TopTools_ShapeMapHasher` — build it once with `add`, then `find_index(&shape)` per query. Use the `_TopTools_ShapeMapHasher` variant, not the bare `IndexedMap_TopoDS_Shape`; that hasher is what gives OCCT's shape-identity semantics.

One correction to what I said last time: I'd suggested adding the `TopTools_*` → `n_collection::*` mapping to PORTING.md. It's already there — the table at line 570 has the exact entry, and line 573 already warns about the hasher variant. So that documentation wasn't missing; it just wasn't consulted. No doc change made.

The other two gaps (`ResD1` accessors, `SLProps` templates) are untouched and still stand as described — both redundant with `BRepAdaptor_Surface::d1`, and `SLProps` in particular needs template-instantiation method generation rather than a config line.

> Actually, I realize completing a class template specialization automatically instantiates all member declarations without needing explicit instantiation — just referencing the type through a typedef forces clang to complete it. > The trick is that a `using` alias alone won't force completion, but adding an explicit instantiation definition or even a `sizeof()` check in the synthetic source will. ## 🤖🔧 Assistant
<details>
<summary>Write tmp/alias_audit.sh</summary>

```bash
#!/bin/bash
# For each OCCT template alias, report how much of it is actually bound.
cd . || exit 1
GEN=crates/opencascade-sys/generated
INC=target/OCCT/include

grep -rhoE "^using [A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]* += +[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_:]*<[^;]*>;" "$INC"/*.hxx 2>/dev/null \
  | sed -E 's/^using ([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*) +=.*/\1/' | sort -u > /tmp/aliases.txt

deadend=0; methods=0; unbound=0
: > /tmp/deadends.txt
while read -r a; do
  # count extern fns for this type, and how many are just lifecycle
  total=$(grep -rhoE "\b${a}_[A-Za-z0-9_]+\(" "$GEN"/ffi_extern_*.rs 2>/dev/null | sort -u | wc -l | tr -d ' ')
  if [ "$total" -eq 0 ]; then
    # not bound at all?
    if grep -qE "pub struct ${a}\b" "$GEN"/ffi_types.rs 2>/dev/null; then
      deadend=$((deadend+1)); echo "$a (type only)" >> /tmp/deadends.txt
    else
      unbound=$((unbound+1))
    fi
  else
    real=$(grep -rhoE "\b${a}_[A-Za-z0-9_]+\(" "$GEN"/ffi_extern_*.rs 2>/dev/null | sort -u \
           | grep -vcE "_(destructor|clone|is_null|get|get_mut|to_handle)\(")
    if [ "$real" -eq 0 ]; then
      deadend=$((deadend+1)); echo "$a ($total lifecycle-only)" >> /tmp/deadends.txt
    else
      methods=$((methods+1))
    fi
  fi
done < /tmp/aliases.txt

echo "template aliases total:        $(wc -l < /tmp/aliases.txt)"
echo "  bound WITH real methods:     $methods"
echo "  bound but DEAD-END (no methods): $deadend"
echo "  not bound at all:            $unbound"
echo ""
echo "=== dead-ends ==="
cat /tmp/deadends.txt

```
</details>

## 🤖 Assistant
<!-- msg_011CdaCYgPJ5Uo7amjmXPhV8 -->

Verified the mechanics end-to-end. Here's the assessment.

## Feasibility: the hard part is already done

I probed this rather than guessing. Two enablers already exist:

1. **The parser builds a synthetic amalgamated TU** ([parser.rs:485](crates/opencascade-binding-generator/src/parser.rs:485)) — just concatenated `#include`s in a string. Injecting `template class Foo<Args>;` lines is a few lines of code.
2. **`template_instantiations` config already exists**, is parsed, and already produces mangled FFI names via `template_alias_name()`.

And the critical unknown — does clang hand back usable instantiated methods? — is a yes. With an explicit instantiation in the TU, clang produces a `ClassTemplateSpecializationDecl` with **21 non-operator methods, fully type-substituted**:

```
Normal      :: const gp_Dir &()
MaxCurvature:: double ()
SetSurface  :: void (const opencascade::handle<Geom_Surface> &)   // Geom alias
SetSurface  :: void (const BRepAdaptor_Surface &)                 // BRep alias
```

No textual substitution, no template-argument bookkeeping — the types come out concrete and are all types the generator already handles (`gp_Dir`, `gp_Vec`, `double`, `handle<T>`). Downstream is entirely reusable: everything from `ClassBindings` onward is keyed on `ParsedClass`, so producing one for a specialization makes C++ wrappers and Rust bindings fall out unchanged.

## The work

| Piece | Effort |
|---|---|
| Emit `template class X<Args>;` into the synthetic TU | trivial |
| Let `parse_class` accept *configured* specializations (it currently bails on `get_template().is_some()` and on `<` in the display name) | small, but needs care to still reject the primary template and unconfigured specializations |
| Map specialization → alias name (`GeomLProp_SLPropsBase<…>` → `GeomLProp_SLProps`) for module/type/FFI naming | **the fiddly part** |
| Toolkit assignment + avoid duplicate definitions across split files | moderate — same problem collections already solved via `own_class_names` |
| Exclude anything the NCollection path already handles | small but essential |
| Graceful skip when a template won't instantiate for given args | small |

Call it **1–2 days**, a few hundred lines net, comparable to the collections auto-discovery work but smaller since codegen is reused. The residual risk sits in naming and de-duplication, not in the clang mechanics — which is the good place for it to sit.

## What it unlocks — with a caveat that matters

I expected this to recover a pile of skipped methods. It doesn't. Of 721 skipped symbols, 442 cite an unresolved template — but **426 of those are `NCollection<...>` with `handle<T>` elements**, which is a different fix (extending collection discovery), and the remaining **16 are `std::optional`/`std::array`**, which is a value-mapping problem. Net skipped signatures recovered by this feature: **~0**.

The value is entirely in the *classes*. Across all 74 template aliases in OCCT's installed headers, **not one currently has a single bound method** — 26 are dead-ends (type + destructor only), 48 aren't bound at all.

| Unlocked | Value |
|---|---|
| `GeomLProp_SLProps` + `BRepLProp_SLProps` | 21 methods each — `Normal`, Gaussian/Mean/Max/Min curvature, `IsUmbilic`, `CurvatureDirections`, `D1U`…`DUV`. The latter isn't bound at all today |
| `math_Vector`, `math_IntegerVector` | entry point to OCCT's math module |
| `Bnd_B2d/B2f/B3d/B3f` | fast bounding boxes |
| 21 × `BRepGraph_*` typed IDs | a module that's currently all opaque |

Being fair about the curvature case: it isn't strictly unreachable today — `BRepAdaptor_Surface::d2()` is bound, so you can assemble the fundamental forms and derive curvature yourself. SLProps gives it directly, with OCCT's degeneracy handling (`IsCurvatureDefined`, `IsUmbilic`) that hand-rolled code tends to get wrong.

## Recommendation

If the goal is **method coverage**, this is the wrong target — handle-element collections is, at 426 skips against what is likely comparable effort, and it's the same shape of problem you already solved once for value-element collections.

Do template instantiation if you specifically want the **differential-geometry API** (curvature/umbilics for fillet, draft, and feature analysis) or the math module. Those are real capability gaps that no amount of collection work reaches — just don't expect the skip count to move.

```
pub struct TemplateInstantiation {
    /// The C++ header that defines the template (for wrappers.cpp includes).
    pub header: String,
    /// The OCCT module this type belongs to (for re-export file placement).
    pub module: String,
    /// Whether this instantiation inherits from Standard_Transient and needs Handle support.
    #[serde(default)]
    pub handle: bool,
    /// Friendly name to bind this instantiation under — normally the OCCT alias
    /// (e.g. `GeomLProp_SLProps` for `GeomLProp_SLPropsBase<occ::handle<Geom_Surface>>`).
    /// Defaults to the mangled `template_alias_name()` spelling when unset.
    #[serde(default)]
    pub alias: Option<String>,
    /// Opt in to full method generation for this instantiation.
    ///
    /// Off by default: most entries here exist only to register a type (and its
    /// Handle) so signatures mentioning it resolve. Turning this on makes the
    /// parser force an explicit instantiation so libclang materializes the
    /// specialization's members, which are then bound like any other class.
    #[serde(default)]
    pub generate_methods: bool,
}

/// The name an instantiation is bound under: the configured `alias` when given,
/// otherwise the mangled spelling.
pub fn instantiation_binding_name(spelling: &str, inst: &TemplateInstantiation) -> String {
    inst.alias
        .clone()
        .unwrap_or_else(|| template_alias_name(spelling))
}
```

```
fn parse_class(entity: &Entity, source_header: &str, verbose: bool) -> Vec<ParsedClass> {
    let name = match entity.get_name() {
        Some(n) => n,
        None => return Vec::new(),
    };
```

**After:**
```
/// `forced_name` binds a class under a caller-chosen name instead of its own.
/// It is set only for configured template instantiations, where the entity is a
/// `ClassTemplateSpecializationDecl` (whose own name is the bare template, e.g.
/// `GeomLProp_SLPropsBase`) and we want the OCCT alias (`GeomLProp_SLProps`).
/// Passing it also waives the template guards below, which otherwise reject every
/// specialization.
fn parse_class(
    entity: &Entity,
    source_header: &str,
    verbose: bool,
    forced_name: Option<&str>,
) -> Vec<ParsedClass> {
    let name = match forced_name {
        Some(n) => n.to_string(),
        None => match entity.get_name() {
            Some(n) => n,
            None => return Vec::new(),
        },
    };
```
</details>

```
    // instantiation libclang leaves the specialization incomplete and exposes no
    // members — merely naming or `sizeof`-ing the alias is not enough. With it,
    // the members appear with template arguments already substituted, so they can
    // be parsed like any ordinary class.
    for req in template_classes {
        synthetic_source.push_str(&format!("template class {};\n", req.spelling));
    }
```

<details>
<summary>Edit crates/opencascade-binding-generator/src/parser.rs</summary>

**Before:**

```
/// Get the canonical path of the file an entity is located in
```

**After:**
```
/// Bind configured template instantiations with a full method set.
///
/// Matching is done through the *alias* declaration rather than by comparing
/// specialization spellings: clang canonicalizes those (expanding default
/// arguments and namespace aliases), so the spelling in config rarely matches
/// verbatim. An alias name is stable and unambiguous, and following it lands on
/// exactly the specialization that `parse_headers` instantiated.
fn parse_template_classes(
    root: &Entity,
    requests: &[TemplateClassRequest],
    filename_to_index: &std::collections::HashMap<&str, usize>,
    results: &mut [ParsedHeader],
    verbose: bool,
) {
    if requests.is_empty() {
        return;
    }
    let by_alias: HashMap<&str, &TemplateClassRequest> =
        requests.iter().map(|r| (r.alias.as_str(), r)).collect();
    let mut bound: HashSet<String> = HashSet::new();

    root.visit_children(|entity, _parent| {
        let kind = entity.get_kind();
        if kind != EntityKind::TypedefDecl && kind != EntityKind::TypeAliasDecl {
            return EntityVisitResult::Recurse;
        }
        let Some(name) = entity.get_name() else {
            return EntityVisitResult::Continue;
        };
        let Some(req) = by_alias.get(name.as_str()).copied() else {
            return EntityVisitResult::Continue;
        };
        if bound.contains(&name) {
            return EntityVisitResult::Continue;
        }
        // alias -> underlying type -> the specialization that declares the members
        let Some(spec) = entity
            .get_typedef_underlying_type()
            .and_then(|t| t.get_declaration())
        else {
            return EntityVisitResult::Continue;
        };
        let Some(&index) = filename_to_index.get(req.header.as_str()) else {
            eprintln!(
                "  warning: template class {} declared in {}, which is not in the parsed header set",
                req.alias, req.header
            );
            return EntityVisitResult::Continue;
        };

        let parsed = parse_class(&spec, &req.header, verbose, Some(&req.alias));
        match parsed.iter().find(|c| c.name == req.alias) {
            Some(c) if c.methods.is_empty() && c.constructors.is_empty() => {
                eprintln!(
                    "  warning: template class {} instantiated but exposed no members",
                    req.alias
                );
            }
            Some(c) => {
                println!(
                    "  Template class {}: {} methods, {} constructors",
                    req.alias,
                    c.methods.len(),
                    c.constructors.len()
                );
            }
            None => {
                eprintln!("  warning: template class {} produced no class", req.alias);
            }
        }
        results[index].classes.extend(parsed);
        bound.insert(name);
        EntityVisitResult::Continue
    });

    for req in requests {
        if !bound.contains(&req.alias) {
            eprintln!(
                "  warning: template class alias {} not found (is `{}` declared in a parsed header?)",
                req.alias, req.alias
            );
        }
    }
}

/// Get the canonical path of the file an entity is located in
```
</details>

**Before:**

```
fn class_type(type_name: &str) -> Type {

```

**After:**
```
fn class_type(type_name: &str) -> Type {
    // While binding a configured template instantiation, template parameters
    // resolve to their concrete arguments. libclang reports members of the primary
    // template only, so without this every member mentioning a parameter would be
    // skipped as an unknown type.
    let key = type_name.trim();
    if let Some(subst) =
        TEMPLATE_PARAM_SUBSTITUTIONS.with(|m| m.borrow().get(key).cloned())
    {
        return subst;
    }
    Type::Class(normalize_occt_alias_type_name(type_name))
}

/// Install template-parameter substitutions for the duration of one class parse.
fn set_template_param_substitutions(subs: HashMap<String, Type>) {
    TEMPLATE_PARAM_SUBSTITUTIONS.with(|m| *m.borrow_mut() = subs);
}

/// Parse a config template-parameter value into a type: `Handle(X)` becomes a
/// handle to X, anything else is a plain class name.
fn parse_template_param_type(spec: &str) -> Type {
    let s = spec.trim();
    match s.strip_prefix("Handle(").and_then(|r| r.strip_suffix(')')) {
        Some(inner) => Type::Handle(inner.trim().to_string()),
        None => Type::Class(s.to_string()),
    }
}
```
</details>

```
                c.constructors.len()

            }
        }
```

```
        let target = spec.get_template().unwrap_or(spec);
        let subs: HashMap<String, Type> = req
            .template_params
            .iter()
            .map(|(param, spec)| (param.clone(), parse_template_param_type(spec)))
            .collect();
        set_template_param_substitutions(subs);
        let parsed = parse_class(&target, &req.header, verbose, Some(&req.alias));
        set_template_param_substitutions(HashMap::new());
```

```
//! Tests for `BRepLProp_SLProps` — the surface differential-geometry API.
//!
//! `GeomLProp_SLProps` / `BRepLProp_SLProps` are aliases for instantiations of the
//! `GeomLProp_SLPropsBase` template, so before template-instantiation support they
//! were bound as opaque types with no methods. These tests check the real numbers,
//! not just that the calls link: a sphere of radius R has Gaussian curvature
//! 1/R^2 and principal curvatures of magnitude 1/R everywhere, and a planar face
//! has zero curvature, so both are exact oracles.

use opencascade_sys::b_rep_adaptor;
use opencascade_sys::b_rep_l_prop::SLProps;
use opencascade_sys::b_rep_prim_api::{MakeBox, MakeSphere};
use opencascade_sys::top_abs;
use opencascade_sys::top_exp;
use opencascade_sys::topo_ds;

/// Second-order properties, with a tight resolution for the degeneracy tests.
const DEGREE: i32 = 2;
const RESOLUTION: f64 = 1e-9;

/// Grab the first face of a shape.
fn first_face(shape: &topo_ds::Shape) -> opencascade_sys::OwnedPtr<topo_ds::Face> {
    let mut explorer = top_exp::Explorer::new_shape_shapeenum2(
        shape,
        top_abs::ShapeEnum::Face,
        top_abs::ShapeEnum::Shape,
    );
    assert!(explorer.more(), "shape should have at least one face");
    topo_ds::face(explorer.current())
}

#[test]
fn sphere_curvature_matches_closed_form() {
    const R: f64 = 5.0;
    let mut sphere = MakeSphere::new_real(R);
    let shape = sphere.as_b_rep_builder_api_make_shape_mut().shape();
    let face = first_face(shape);

    let adaptor = b_rep_adaptor::Surface::new_face(&face);
    let mut props = SLProps::new_surface_int_real(&adaptor, DEGREE, RESOLUTION);

    // Sample a few (u,v) away from the poles/seam.
    for (u, v) in [(0.5, 0.0), (1.0, 0.4), (2.5, -0.3)] {
        props.set_parameters(u, v);

        assert!(
            props.is_curvature_defined(),
            "curvature should be defined at ({u}, {v})"
        );

        // Gaussian curvature of a sphere is 1/R^2 regardless of orientation.
        let gaussian = props.gaussian_curvature();
        assert!(
            (gaussian - 1.0 / (R * R)).abs() < 1e-9,
            "gaussian curvature at ({u}, {v}) was {gaussian}, expected {}",
            1.0 / (R * R)
        );

        // Principal curvatures both have magnitude 1/R (sign follows the normal).
        for k in [props.max_curvature(), props.min_curvature()] {
            assert!(
                (k.abs() - 1.0 / R).abs() < 1e-9,
                "principal curvature {k} should have magnitude {}",
                1.0 / R
            );
        }

        // |mean| = 1/R on a sphere.
        let mean = props.mean_curvature().abs();
        assert!(
            (mean - 1.0 / R).abs() < 1e-9,
            "mean curvature magnitude at ({u}, {v}) was {mean}, expected {}",
            1.0 / R
        );
    }
}

/// On a sphere centred at the origin the unit normal at a point is parallel to
/// the position vector, which also confirms Value() and Normal() agree.
#[test]
fn sphere_normal_is_radial() {
    const R: f64 = 3.0;
    let mut sphere = MakeSphere::new_real(R);
    let shape = sphere.as_b_rep_builder_api_make_shape_mut().shape();
    let face = first_face(shape);

    let adaptor = b_rep_adaptor::Surface::new_face(&face);
    let mut props = SLProps::new_surface_int_real(&adaptor, DEGREE, RESOLUTION);
    props.set_parameters(1.0, 0.3);

    assert!(props.is_normal_defined(), "normal should be defined");

    let p = props.value();
    let (px, py, pz) = (p.x(), p.y(), p.z());
    let len = (px * px + py * py + pz * pz).sqrt();
    assert!(
        (len - R).abs() < 1e-9,
        "point should lie on the sphere, got radius {len}"
    );

    let n = props.normal();
    // |dot(n, p/|p|)| == 1 when the normal is radial (sign depends on face sense).
    let dot = (n.x() * px + n.y() * py + n.z() * pz) / len;
    assert!(
        (dot.abs() - 1.0).abs() < 1e-9,
        "normal should be radial; |cos| was {}",
        dot.abs()
    );
}

/// A planar face is flat: both principal curvatures, and hence Gaussian and mean
/// curvature, are zero, and it is umbilic.
#[test]
fn planar_face_is_flat() {
    let mut boxy = MakeBox::new_real3(10.0, 10.0, 10.0);
    let shape = boxy.as_b_rep_builder_api_make_shape_mut().shape();
    let face = first_face(shape);

    let adaptor = b_rep_adaptor::Surface::new_face(&face);
    let mut props = SLProps::new_surface_int_real(&adaptor, DEGREE, RESOLUTION);
    props.set_parameters(5.0, 5.0);

    assert!(props.is_curvature_defined(), "curvature defined on a plane");
    assert!(
        props.gaussian_curvature().abs() < 1e-12,
        "plane should have zero gaussian curvature, got {}",
        props.gaussian_curvature()
    );
    assert!(
        props.mean_curvature().abs() < 1e-12,
        "plane should have zero mean curvature, got {}",
        props.mean_curvature()
    );
    // Every direction is principal on a plane, so OCCT reports it as umbilic.
    assert!(props.is_umbilic(), "a plane is umbilic");
}

```
</details>

```
/// Second-order properties, with a tight resolution for the degeneracy tests.
const DEGREE: i32 = 2;
const RESOLUTION: f64 = 1e-9;

/// Build a surface adaptor over the first face of a shape. The explorer only has
/// to outlive the borrowed face, not the adaptor, which owns its copy.
fn first_face_adaptor(
    shape: &topo_ds::Shape,
) -> opencascade_sys::OwnedPtr<b_rep_adaptor::Surface> {
    let explorer = top_exp::Explorer::new_shape_shapeenum2(
        shape,
        top_abs::ShapeEnum::Face,
        top_abs::ShapeEnum::Shape,
    );
    assert!(explorer.more(), "shape should have at least one face");
    b_rep_adaptor::Surface::new_face(topo_ds::face_shape(explorer.current()))
}
```

```
### Collection Types

Collections are **auto-discovered** from the canonical \`NCollection_*<...>\` template
spellings the parsed API references (\`collections.rs\`: \`discover_collections\`). Each
instantiation whose element/value types are bindable is materialized in the \`n_collection\`
module under a canonical mangled name (\`NCollection_List<TopoDS_Shape>\` →
\`n_collection::List_TopoDS_Shape\`) with:
- a C++ \`typedef <spelling> <mangled>;\` bridge, so the wrappers use the short mangled name
- full element methods per kind (list/sequence/array/map/data-map), primitive-aware
  (scalar elements/values are passed by value rather than pointer)
- a C++ iterator struct + \`TypeNameIterator_next()\` function and a real \`Iterator\` impl
  (\`iter()\` — usable in \`for\` loops and adapters, via a blanket \`Iterator for OwnedPtr<I>\`)
- for handle kinds (\`HArray1\`/\`HSequence\`): \`to_handle\` plus \`get\`/\`get_mut\` and the
  handle lifetime machinery on the generated \`Handle_*\` type

The canonical names are the same ones method signatures resolve to, so a returned
\`&NCollection_List<TopoDS_Shape>\` and the collection's own methods refer to one type.

In overload suffixes, collection parameters use a short registered token (\`imap_shape\`,
\`arr1_pnt\`, \`harr1_pnt\`, \`dmap_shape_int\` — dropping the hasher argument) rather than the
full mangled type, so an overloaded \`TopExp::MapShapes\` reads
\`map_shapes_shape_shapeenum_imap_shape\` instead of
\`map_shapes_shape_shapeenum_indexedmap_topods_shape_toptools_shapemaphasher\`.

### Standard Streams (iostream)
```
</details>


**After:**
````markdown
### Template Class Instantiations

Class templates are normally skipped, so an OCCT alias for an instantiation
(`using GeomLProp_SLProps = GeomLProp_SLPropsBase<occ::handle<Geom_Surface>>;`) binds as
an opaque type with a destructor and no methods. A `[template_instantiations]` entry with
`generate_methods = true` binds one with a full method set:

```toml
"GeomLProp_SLPropsBase<occ::handle<Geom_Surface>>" = { header = "GeomLProp_SLProps.hxx", \
    module = "GeomLProp", alias = "GeomLProp_SLProps", generate_methods = true, \
    template_params = { SurfaceType = "Handle(Geom_Surface)" } }
```

- `alias` is the name the class is bound under, and must be a real C++ alias — generated
  wrappers use it verbatim as the type spelling. Defaults to the mangled spelling.
- `template_params` maps each template parameter to its concrete argument, either
  `Handle(X)` or a plain class name.

Two libclang details drive the implementation (`parser.rs`: `parse_template_classes`):

1. A specialization's members only materialize if something forces instantiation, so the
   synthetic TU gets a `template class <spelling>;` line per entry. Naming or `sizeof`-ing
   the alias is *not* enough — the specialization stays incomplete.
2. Even then, libclang exposes only template-argument references on the specialization
   cursor; the members live on the **primary template**, with parameters unsubstituted.
   (Clang's own AST has the substituted members — libclang's cursor API is a lossier view.)
   So members are parsed from the primary template while `template_params` substitutes the
   parameters, which is why that mapping is required rather than inferred.

Members mentioning an unlisted parameter are skipped as unknown types; the many members
that never mention one bind either way.

### Standard Streams (iostream)
````
</details>

## 🤖🔧 Assistant
<details>
<summary>Write tmp/commit-template-classes.txt</summary>

```
Generate methods for configured template class instantiations

OCCT 8.0 turned several plain classes into aliases for template instantiations —
`using GeomLProp_SLProps = GeomLProp_SLPropsBase<occ::handle<Geom_Surface>>;`.
The parser skips class templates, so these bound as opaque types carrying a
destructor and nothing else. Across all 74 such aliases in the installed headers,
not one had a single bound method.

Add an opt-in path for binding an instantiation with a full method set. A
`[template_instantiations]` entry gains `generate_methods`, `alias`, and
`template_params`; everything already in that table keeps its existing
type-registration-only behaviour.

Two libclang details shape the implementation:

  1. A specialization's members only materialize when something forces
     instantiation, so parse_headers appends `template class <spelling>;` to the
     synthetic TU. Naming the alias or taking its sizeof is not enough — the
     specialization stays incomplete with zero members.

  2. Even with that, libclang exposes only template-argument references on the
     specialization cursor. The members live on the *primary template*, with
     parameters unsubstituted. Clang's own AST does carry the substituted
     members; libclang's cursor API is a lossier view of it.

So members are parsed from the primary template, with `template_params` supplying
the concrete argument for each parameter (`Handle(X)` or a class name). The
substitution is installed as a thread-local consulted by `class_type()`, the
single point where a named type becomes `Type::Class`. Members mentioning an
unlisted parameter are skipped as unknown types, which is harmless for the many
members that never mention one. Instantiations are matched through their alias
declaration rather than by comparing specialization spellings, since clang
canonicalizes those (expanding default arguments and namespace aliases).

Bind GeomLProp_SLProps and BRepLProp_SLProps as the first users: 21 methods and 3
constructors each, covering the surface differential-geometry API — Normal,
Gaussian/mean/max/min curvature, IsUmbilic, CurvatureDirections, tangents, and
D1U..DUV — with no remaining skips. Both `SetSurface` overloads and the
surface-taking constructors bind to the substituted type, so the objects are
actually constructible: `Handle_Geom_Surface` for the Geom alias and
`b_rep_adaptor::Surface` for the BRep one.

Existing signatures improve as a side effect: `GeomPlate`, `LocalAnalysis` and
`BRepFill` methods that returned `ffi_types::GeomLProp_SLProps` now return
`geom_l_prop::SLProps`, so callers can use the result instead of holding an
opaque pointer.

Note this recovers no previously-skipped symbols (the count stays at 721) — these
members were never parsed, so they were never recorded as skips. The gain is the
classes themselves becoming usable.

tests/surface_local_props.rs checks the numbers rather than just linkage: a
sphere of radius R has Gaussian curvature 1/R^2 and principal curvatures of
magnitude 1/R, its normal is radial, and a planar face is flat and umbilic.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 <noreply@anthropic.com>

```
</details>
